  ### Feasibility (from a read-only research subagent; I checked the BCG method in the report PDF myself)
  - **Trust Project:** a news-credibility standard with 8 "Trust Indicators" and 300+ partner sites. It publishes no time series, and its site is all rights reserved. Not a usable proxy.
  - **BCG Trust Index:** four dimensions (competence, fairness, transparency, resilience).
    - Data: news plus Twitter.
    - Mentions: found with a 200+ word lexicon that BCG does not publish.
    - Sentiment: the Quid engine.
    - Score per source: (positive − negative) / (positive + negative), weighted by sentences × potential impressions.
    - Universe: about 1,100 companies over $20B, 2018–2021.
    - Updates: no edition after 2022 found, only a 2024 reuse on 177 companies.
  
  **BCG extension plan:**
  - Mentions:
    - GDELT DOC `artlist` mode for article URLs, then article text;
    - the Hacker News corpus as the social source;
    - the earnings-call corpus for what investors say.
  - A published, versioned regex lexicon per dimension, built from BCG's examples.
  - The two NLI witnesses (DeBERTa and ModernBERT) for polarity and dimension. A positive needs both at ≥ 0.5, and results stay proposals until checked against a human gold set.
  - The peer set (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, Microsoft, Meta) as a relative benchmark.
  - **Proprietary parts we can't replace:** Quid, audience and impression data, X/Twitter access, and the full lexicon. The result would be a "BCG-style" index, not BCG's.
  
  **Other free proxies:**
  
  | Source | Terms | Cadence | AI / tech coverage |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | V-Dem v16 | CC BY-SA 4.0 (share-alike, so not CC0) | Annual | None |
  | Freedom House | Noncommercial use only | Annual | None |
  | Pew trust in government (17%, Sept 2025) | Excerpts only | Irregular | Separate AI surveys |
  | Gallup Confidence in Institutions | Reuse terms not verified | Annual | "Large technology companies" since 2020: 32% then, 20% in June 2026 |
  | Edelman Trust Barometer | Pages returned 403; not verified | Annual | Tech and AI-company items |
  
  No free source measures trust in Anthropic itself; the doc says so.
  
  ### Judgment calls
  - **DOC queries are not filtered by language,** so machine-translated articles can match.
  - **TV is limited to `market:"National"`** with stations combined, and the TV co-mention queries use a single 15-second clip as the proximity window.
  - **Hedged values keep the number** in `percent` (e.g. "roughly 10%"). Bounds, ranges and verbal fractions get no number.
  - **Wave labels are kept as each report states them,** without reconciling them.
  - **Rooney PDFs and GDELT raw responses are not redistributed;** they are pinned by hash. Feasibility sources are cited by URL, not pinned.
  - **Pins:** `blake3==1.0.11`, because 1.0.5 has no Python 3.14 wheel. TSVs are written unquoted (`QUOTE_NONE`).
  - **Writing checks not run:** your global CLAUDE.md requires the buzz-saw-editor and brand-voice checks for argument prose, and I did not run them on the doc.

  ### Code that contradicts the docs or itself (not fixed)
  1. **Window clipping.** Method doc §2 says a window over 1,500 characters "is clipped to 600 characters around each match". In the code, the pieces around nearby matches merge, so 856 windows are still over 1,500 characters; the longest is 7,894.
  2. **Window merging.** §2 says "overlapping windows … are merged". The code (`lo <= spans[-1][1] + 1`) also merges windows that only touch.
  3. **Codebook licence.** You asked for CC0. But AGENTS.md says repo content is CC0 except `corpus/*/`, and `corpus/earnings-calls/README.md` says the folder "is not covered by the repository's CC0 dedication". The codebooks live in that folder, and the README names no licence for them. I wrote CC0 and recorded the conflict in `terms_notes`.
  4. **Same column name, two definitions.** `calls_mentioning_ai` in `report/by-year.tsv` counts calls with any window, with no witness condition. In `report/by-firm.tsv` it requires both witnesses to agree on `about_ai`. Both column descriptions say so.
  5. **Two definitions of "AI as a risk".** `ec_risk_series.py` (`ai-risk-by-year`, the TTM ranking and quotes) uses the `ai_risk` flag alone. `ec_report.py` (by-year, by-firm, risk-types) requires `about_ai` and `ai_risk`.
  6. **Commands in `dag.yml` would not rebuild the committed scores.** The witness steps run `uv run scripts/ec_classify.py --model …`, which defaults to float32 on a local device. The committed run records show `scripts/ec_classify_modal.py` on Modal L40S, bfloat16, batch 128, and shard tags carry the backend. Method doc §8 has the right Modal commands.
  7. **Repository URL mismatch.** The user agent in `ec_extract.py` names `github.com/ethicalcapital/anthropic-pbc`; the git remote is `github.com/srvo/anthropic-pbc`.
  8. **"Verbatim" is true only for this build.** The docstrings call `risk-quotes-ttm.tsv` and `oversight-questions.tsv` verbatim, but the code replaces newlines and tabs with spaces. No window or question in this build contains either, so they are verbatim today; the descriptions say so.

  ## Judgment calls
  
  **Snapshot metadata**
  1. **Dataset count.** You said 16 datasets; there are 15. MANIFEST and CITATIONS both have 15 dataset ids, and the 18 files match. /data lists 17, of which Cyber vulnerabilities is rebuilt in `corpus/cve/` and ChatGPT usage is excluded. The description says 15.
  2. **`date_published` and `version_producer` are null, with reasons.** Epoch gives a per-file "Updated" date (2026-03-13 to 2026-11-04) and no version. These dates are in `datasets:` and in MANIFEST.
  3. **`url_download`** refers to MANIFEST's url column, since there are 18 URLs.
  4. **Extra keys** beyond the schema: `files`, `citations_file`, and `datasets:` (`id`, `url`, `updated`, `citation`). A script check confirmed every citation, url and updated date matches CITATIONS.tsv and MANIFEST.tsv exactly.
  5. **`terms_notes`** covers CC BY 4.0 (Epoch's wording), the 67 `_external` benchmark tables keeping their own licences, the ChatGPT/YouGov exclusion (notice quoted), and the UN Comtrade and national-agency acknowledgements on the chip imports page.
  6. **`personal_data: verbatim_public_text`**, with a note. The ai-models tables have an Authors column, and the polling microdata has de-identified respondent rows with demographics and open text (Epoch redacted two answers, flagged in `QF1_redacted`). The enum has no value for de-identified survey microdata, so the schema may want one.
  7. **No no-AI-ingestion notice** appears in the Ipsos questionnaire, methodology or `.sav`. The YouGov notice applies only to ChatGPT usage.
  
  **Dataset level and inventory.tsv**
  8. **`status: held`.** The license is CC BY 4.0, with a note that the folder is outside the repo's CC0 dedication.
  9. **inventory.tsv** is documented from `scripts/epoch_data.py`. Key is `[dataset, file, member]`. `member` is an empty string on the six rows where the pinned file is a plain CSV, and I treat that empty string as a value. `blake3` is not unique because `ai-chip-sales/chip_types.csv` and `ai-data-centers/chip_types.csv` are byte-identical.
  
  **Key choices**
  10. **ai-research-use keys are Epoch's stated grain**, such as `[field, subfield, period]`, with `subfield` empty on whole-field rows. Epoch says "Rows with an empty subfield cover the whole field", so I treat the empty string as a value, not as missing. The fork had written null here; I changed it so these tables do not add gaps. The undocumented `source` column is left out of the key.
  11. **Other keys where Epoch states none** were chosen by uniqueness tests, recorded in each `primary_key_note`:
      - ai-chip-components and ai-chip-imports: natural composites such as `[Quarter, Designer, Chip type]` and `[Quarter, Country]`.
      - ai-companies: `[Id]`, or `[Id, Category]` / `[Id, Date]` where Id repeats.
      - Per-benchmark tables: `[id]`, although `id` itself is undocumented.
      - Polling summary: `[question_number, demographic, demographic_value, response]`, which is one column wider than the minimal unique key.
  
  **How strictly "Epoch defines it" was read**
  12. **Descriptions assembled from Epoch's definitions.** This is the loosest call. In ai-chip-components, imports and users, Epoch has no field tables. Where Epoch defines the quantity a column names (for example logic wafers, or H100e arrivals) plus what its P5/median/P95 or low/high mean, the description combines those quoted definitions. A stricter reading would turn roughly 150 of these columns unknown.
  13. **Field names that differ trivially from the docs** were mapped, with `source_field` set:
      - case and spacing differences
      - "(from X)" lookup suffixes
      - "Source 1 (through Source n)"
      - Epoch's generic "Notes fields" definition, applied to the ai-models notes columns
  14. **Less trivial renames, also with `source_field` set:**
      - ml_hardware: "Memory (bytes)" mapped to "Memory size per board (byte)", and "Transistors (millions)" mapped to "Number of transistors (millions)".
      - ai-chip-owners `cumulative_by_chip_type`: "Number of Units (median)" uses the field of the same name from the designer section of the same records page.
      - benchmarks: "Logs" mapped to the Airtable `logs` field.
      - polling: `weight` and the summary table's `se` are documented from the page's text about weights and standard errors.
  15. **Cross-file reuse** happened in exactly one place. `ai-data-centers/chip_types.csv` uses the ai-chip-sales "Chip types" definitions: the two files are byte-identical, and Epoch's data-centers downloads page lists the file as "AI chip sales: Chip types". No other definitions were borrowed across datasets or tables. For example, owners "Incomplete" and sales "Last Modified" stay unknown, and so do the two undocumented ai-chip-sales cumulative tables.
  16. **Benchmarks.**
      - "Best score (across scorers)" is documented only in the 14 tables that `benchmark_metadata.csv` points to as `score_column`.
      - `processed_data_for_eci.csv` is treated as the output of eci-public's `prepare_benchmark_data`, because its header is identical to that function's return columns.
      - The file has both `model` and `Model` columns, documented separately.
  17. **Polling labels.** SPSS labels are used verbatim, with whitespace collapsed. Labels that only restate the variable name, and the Qualtrics placeholder labels on `Q6A_*_TH`, are declared unknown. Open-text columns (18, including QF1 and every `*_TEXT`) are marked `verbatim: true`. `values` holds Epoch's value labels where there are 12 or fewer.
  18. **Units** are set only where Epoch's header or documentation states one: USD, W, GW, MW, FLOP, H100e, %, wafers, seconds, `YYYY-MM-DD` for dates, `YYYY-MM` for ai-research-use `period`.
      - Header-stated units are kept even on some columns whose description is unknown, for example the chiller and cooling-tower dimensions and the FP8/FP4 performance columns.
      - "% of largest cluster when first operational" has unit `ratio`, because Epoch defines it as one value divided by another and the stored values run from 0.01 to 1.0. A `note:` says so.
  19. **Extra column keys** beyond the schema: `source_field`, `note` and `values_note`. The validator accepts them.
  20. **Contradictions with the values are recorded in `note:` and left unresolved.** For example, gpu-clusters defines "Energy Efficiency" as a log, but the stored values are not logs.
  
  Scratchpad working files (generators, field extractions, fetched docs) are in /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-srvo-dev-anthropic-pbc/c1e11076-829e-4b02-9641-6b48b095d475/scratchpad/. To rebuild the meadow file run `assemble.py`, to re-run the gap check run `metacheck.py --with-meta`, and to re-test keys and recount columns run `verifyall.py`.
</agent-message>

Only `deck/cve-ai-credit.svg` is tracked. `deck/deck.template.md:88`, `DECK.md:88` and `docs/report-build.md:91` all cite `deck/STAMP.json`, which is untracked.   2. **The seal tooling the docs point to is not committed.** `AGENTS.md:77` (HEAD) says to run `uv run scripts/seal.py check --allow-unsealed` and to read README "Maintaining the seal". `seal.py` is untracked, and the HEAD README (155 lines) has no such section. `docs/bert-enrichment-spec.md:306` cites `seal.py` too. `index.html` is tracked, but the script that regenerates it is not.
  3. **Three praxis/descartes docs were copied in with their original repo's paths.**
     - `docs/praxis-white-paper.md` has about 45 links to things that don't exist here: `../src/model.rs`, `../Cargo.toml`, `../claims/*.tsv`, `../prompts/distill.md`, `../artifacts/stage1/…`, `docs/enclave-capital-introduction.md`, `docs/history.md`, `docs/tooling.md`, `docs/endowment.md`. Its links also use `file.md:N` targets, which are dead on GitHub. Line 534 cites `README.md:350` (README has 155 lines).
     - `docs/descartes-spec.md:487-499` links `docs/primitives.md` from inside `docs/`, which resolves to `docs/docs/…`. Lines 98, 156–164 and 414 describe `.descartes/`, `claims/` and Cargo, which this repo doesn't have.
     - `docs/praxis-standard.md:5,76,107-144` has the same problem.
     - None of these three docs exist in `../praxis`. They describe Praxis but live here, so neither repo is their authoritative home.
  4. **The risk-factor corpus is listed but cannot be rebuilt.** `corpus/datasets.tsv` gives `corpus_dir` as `—`, and `DATASETS.md:30` shows it with no pin. `scripts/rf_extract.py`, `rf_loss.py` and `rf_query.py` are in no `dag.yml` step. It has no `MANIFEST.tsv`, and its bulk sits in `.rfcorpus/`. `docs/risk-factor-corpus.md:13` still claims "v1 · rf-extract-v1".
  
  ### Medium
  
  5. **The roadmap marks built work as in progress.** `docs/corpus-roadmap.md:3-4` says nothing is built unless it links a method doc. Line 16 says the Epoch snapshot is "in progress", but `corpus/epoch/` has 74 files and an attestation. Lines 54–56 say democracy lands "when it lands", but `docs/democracy-proxies.md` and `corpus/democracy/` exist and are in the DAG. The roadmap was last touched in e121992 and never updated.
  6. **The enrichment spec's plan section is stale.** `docs/bert-enrichment-spec.md:304` says "At committed HEAD this repo holds documents only". It cites `scripts/enrich_stage2.py` (missing), `frameworks/promise-act-v0.1.md`, `sources/MANIFEST.tsv` and `gold/pilot-v0.1.tsv`, none of which are tracked. §§1–9 are frozen by design, but a newcomer can't tell the plan was superseded; it needs a pointer to §10.
  7. **The Epoch corpus can't be verified by a stranger.** `corpus/epoch/README.md` says the gitignored `.epochcorpus/` "is the only one that verifies against this manifest". That contradicts AGENTS "rebuildable by a stranger", and nothing publishes those bytes.
  8. **The edition build is outside the DAG.** `build_edition.py`, `display.py` and `test_edition_controls.py` are in no step, and nothing checks that DECK.md is fresh. `datasets_md.py check` covers only DATASETS.md.
  9. **`.gitignore` gaps** (`git check-ignore` returns nothing for these):
     - `scripts/__pycache__/` and `.claude/worktrees/` are not ignored.
     - `.claude/settings.json` is tracked, although AGENTS says "never stage `.claude/`".
     - `.entire/settings.json` is tracked.
  10. **The README is not an entry point.** The HEAD README covers only the paper and the earnings-call corpus. It never mentions AGENTS.md, DATASETS.md, `dag.yml`, the Praxis checkout, the other six corpora, `proposals/` or the deck. Nothing describes the full pin → build → verify → witness → report → edition chain. A newcomer has to put it together from AGENTS, DATASETS (generated), `bert-enrichment-spec` §5/§9 and `report-build.md`, which takes more than 10 minutes.
  
  ### Low
  
  11. AGENTS says "Cite data in docs by step URI", but no `docs/*.md` or `corpus/*/README.md` contains a single `snapshot://` or `data://` URI.
  12. Bare `OPENQUESTIONS Q2/Q13` references in about 8 `.meta.yml` files and `bert-enrichment-spec.md:504` don't say the file is in the Praxis repo. `report-build.md:72` does say so. Every `.meta.yml` header cites `praxis/datasets/metadata.schema.yml` correctly.
  13. The repo is 110 MB tracked and 123 MiB packed, after only 53 commits. That includes 27 parquet files, with model-output parquets up to 7 MB each and `window-flags.tsv` at 5.5 MB. Each re-witness adds megabytes of history; consider shipping these as release assets with hashes.
  14. Epoch's method doc is `corpus/epoch/README.md` (per `datasets.tsv`), not a `docs/` file. That breaks "one method doc per corpus in docs/".
  15. Uncommitted work from other sessions exists: index.html, README, the paper, `frameworks/`, `sources/`, `docs/figures/`, `scripts/enrich_*`, `seal.py`, `.githooks`, `.github`, the `deck/*.svg` files and `delete-after-reading.md`. Findings 1 and 2 may close once that work lands.
  
  ## Proposed target layout
  
  ```
  README.md        entry point: what this is, the 6-stage flow diagram, links to AGENTS/DATASETS/paper/deck
  AGENTS.md        conventions only; tools live in ../praxis/datasets
  DATASETS.md      generated; the only catalog (roadmap keeps unbuilt sources only)
  dag.yml          + an edition step (build_edition.py) and an rf step, or drop risk-factors from datasets.tsv
  corpus/<id>/     MANIFEST + README + small tables; bulk stays in .<id>corpus/
  proposals/       witness output (consider release assets for parquets)
  docs/corpora/    one method doc per corpus (move epoch's here)
  docs/paper/      contrapunctus, risk-governance-index, attest, report-build
  deck/            template + every build output, committed together, checked by --check
  praxis-*, descartes-spec, primitives, circles, epistemics, strictness → move to ../praxis/docs
  ```
  
  ## Verdict
  
  The data layer is in good shape. `dag.yml`, the lock, the per-step metadata and DATASETS.md agree and pass every check, and no tracked doc points at the old in-repo `dag.py`, `datasets_md.py`, `OPENQUESTIONS.md` or `PASTEBIN.md` paths. The documentation around it has not kept up. The two outputs a reader sees first, the sealed paper and the deck, depend on tooling (`seal.py`, the deck SVGs, `STAMP.json`) that exists only as uncommitted work in the tree. The committed DECK.md is an older hand-built deck with the codebook mix the edition build was written to prevent. About a third of `docs/` is Praxis/Descartes material carrying roughly 60 dead paths from another repo. The roadmap still calls two finished corpora "in progress". A stranger can rebuild the corpora from AGENTS.md but cannot rebuild the paper seal or the deck from HEAD, and cannot learn the full flow from the README.
</agent-message>

  No accidental exposure. All scans printed file:line and pattern names only. The three email hits were checked by domain only. No credential files or environment dumps were read. The only network call fetched the defeatbeta Hugging Face card metadata to confirm its licence.
  
  ## Findings, ranked
  
  **HIGH**
  
  1. **Verbatim Yahoo-sourced transcripts are redistributed on a licence that probably cannot cover them.** The committed files are:
     - `corpus/earnings-calls/ai-paragraphs.parquet`: 28,602 verbatim paragraphs with speaker names.
     - `ai-windows.parquet`: 38,600 rows with text.
     - `ai-questions.parquet`: 4,288 full analyst turns.
  
     The only licence is the uploader's `odc-by` tag on HF. The defeatbeta card says the data is "sourced from Yahoo! Finance … via publicly available APIs … intended for research and educational purposes". ODC-BY covers database rights. It cannot grant copyright in transcripts that Yahoo or its transcript vendor owns, and Yahoo's terms bar redistribution. `corpus/earnings-calls/README.md` ("Attribution and terms") relies on the HF tag alone. This is the largest legal exposure in the repo. Storing ids and hashes plus rebuild-from-pin, as the HN corpus does, would remove it.
  
  2. **The same transcript text sits outside the licence carve-out, so it is implicitly CC0.**
     - `proposals/earnings-calls/codebook-v0.1/report/risk-quotes-ttm.tsv` has a `quote` column with speaker names.
     - `proposals/earnings-calls/codebook-questions-v0.1/report/oversight-questions.tsv` has a `question` column.
     - `gold/earnings-calls/*/sample.tsv` has a `text` column.
  
     AGENTS.md:10 says "Repo content is CC0 except `corpus/*/`", and README (License section, line 148) carves out only `corpus/earnings-calls/`. That publishes third-party words under a public-domain dedication.
  
  3. **DECK.md publishes rates that the repo's own spec says may not leave `proposals/`.** `docs/bert-enrichment-spec.md` §10 (around lines 413–416 and 437–439) says "No count below is citable as a rate of anything except witness agreement" and sets a gate: precision and recall per hypothesis "before any rate leaves `proposals/`". `gold/earnings-calls/codebook-v0.1/score.tsv:1` says "0 of 240 sample rows coded". The deck still captions rates as facts ("12.2% of 2026 S&P 500 calls", "137 firms"). A small grey disclaimer tag does not cure captions written as findings.
  
  4. **The dialogue-space slide makes a cross-source level comparison that the spec explicitly disowns.** The spec's HN entry says HN type shares "are therefore not comparable to calls in level, only in rough order". It also says a spot check found about half of HN `regulation` windows off-topic. The deck slide nonetheless compares levels: 10% executives vs 39% HN vs 61% Wikipedia.
     - The 61% for Wikipedia is partly built in. The 14 new v0.2 dimensions were read from Wikipedia's AI-risk headings and then scored on those same AI-risk articles.
     - The executive base is 179 units, so its cells rest on a handful of passages.
  
  **MEDIUM**
  
  5. **Forking paths.** The public deck uses `v0.2-draft`, whose wording is unreviewed (OPENQUESTIONS Q2), rather than v0.1. The two give different headline numbers: committed v0.1 `risk-ranking-ttm.tsv` has Cyber 39 and capex 27, while the deck has 40 and 26.
     - The threshold of 0.5 with both witnesses agreeing is not pre-registered and has no sensitivity range. Spec §3 and §6.2 require both and call an unregistered threshold "threshold-shoppable".
     - Witness agreement is weak on several risk types: κ 0.26–0.41 for disruption, capex, bias, privacy, safety_ethics and governance (`report/agreement.tsv`).
     - bf16 scores move with batch composition (spec §10, v0.2 entry).
  
  6. **The intent slide picks an extreme pair.** "21.2% sells vs 0.8% controls" sets the broadest commercial category against the narrowest governance category: 13 questions, κ 0.48. The spec lists the other governance intents: security or privacy 3.0% and risks 2.9%. A summed comparison is still lopsided but honest.
  
  7. **Licence metadata is inconsistent.** `.zenodo.json:9` (`"license": "cc-zero"`) and `CITATION.cff:19` (`CC0-1.0`) label the whole deposit CC0. The corpora are actually ODC-BY, CC BY-SA 4.0, CC BY, CVE terms and Notre Dame copyright. A Zenodo upload would mislicense them.
  
  8. **Survivorship and partial year.** The earnings universe is today's S&P 500 (pinned Wikipedia revision). 2026 runs to 11-04, and calendar-year binning mixes fiscal quarters. The caveats are stated on the last slide only, not beside the trend chart.
  
  9. **Attribution conflict for a registered investment adviser.** README ("Author") says "Personal research; not published … by Ethical Capital". But `corpus/codebooks/codebook-v0.2-draft.meta.yml` has "producer: Ethical Capital" and "citation_full: Ethical Capital …". SEC user-agent strings carry firm emails (`scripts/rf_extract.py:51`, `scripts/rf_loss.py:49`, `scripts/democracy_proxies.py:62`; business addresses, not secrets). A firm-attributed critique of a named company may trigger marketing-rule review.
  
  **LOW**
  
  10. **HN corpus complies.** `windows.parquet` holds ids, offsets and BLAKE3 hashes; the proposals parquets hold `window_id`/`hypothesis_id`/`p_entail`; `examples-ttm.tsv` holds item ids and URLs. No comment text or usernames are stored. Residual risk: item ids resolve to authors, and the reports label individual comments with risk types.
  11. **CVE, Epoch, Wikipedia and Rooney are fine.**
     - CVE stores no record text.
     - The 67 Epoch `*_external.csv` third-party tables are correctly left out (0 tracked), and YouGov data is excluded.
     - Wikipedia carries the CC BY-SA ShareAlike notice.
     - Rooney uses short quotes only.
  12. **Secrets and infrastructure.** No AWS, GitHub, HF, `sk-`, Slack, JWT, PEM, credentialled DSN or key-assignment patterns were found. No private hostnames, tailnet IPs or home paths were found. `docs/bert-enrichment-spec.md:559` names the build host's timezone (America/Denver), which is harmless. `.claude/settings.json` and `.entire/settings.json` are tracked; they hold only hook commands, but this contradicts the AGENTS.md "never stage `.claude/`" rule.
  13. **Fairness of the paper.** The deck's §365(b)/§367 summary is accurate. The paper covers the Long-Term Benefit Trust (14 mentions) and gives explicit per-section falsifiers (`docs/anthropic-contrapunctus.md:713, 743, 774, 811, 848`). The CVE slide fairly says it "cannot confirm or refute" 33,000+. "Legally toothless" is opinion, and its basis is disclosed. I found nothing defamatory. The deck's argument slide omits the trust, which a hostile reader will call selective.
  
  ## Weakest inferential link
  
  The lead chart (DECK.md:58) says "AI framed as a risk: 12.2% of 2026 calls … against at most 0.6% in any year before 2023". That is a call-level "any window positive" metric built on unvalidated labels where both models must agree. Calls with an AI mention grew from about 1 AI window per call in 2022 to about 5.7 in 2026. So with any constant false-positive rate, the call-level rate rises with the volume of AI talk alone.
  
  The per-AI-window risk rate is nearly flat from 2022 to 2024 (`proposals/earnings-calls/codebook-v0.1/report/by-year.tsv`, fiscal years, v0.1):
  
  | Fiscal year | Risk windows | AI windows | Rate |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 2022 | 3 | 385 | 0.8% |
  | 2023 | 22 | 2,175 | 1.0% |
  | 2024 | 33 | 3,317 | 1.0% |
  | 2025 | 66 | 4,324 | 1.5% |
  | 2026 | 191 | 5,875 | 3.3% |
  
  So most of the pre/post-2023 contrast is AI-mention volume multiplied by an unmeasured error rate, not a shift in framing. Any real framing change shows up only in 2025–26, and even that is unverified until the 240-row gold sample is coded.
  
  ## Verdict
  
  The repo is unusually honest in its internals. It states denominators, logs agreement, admits the bf16 non-reproducibility and the bio_chem wording failure, keeps HN text out, and leaves Epoch's external tables out. But the public deck outruns the method: it states witness proposals as rates, compares levels across sources that the spec says are not comparable, and leads with a call-level trend confounded by mention volume. All of this sits on a draft codebook with no gold labels. Legally, the serious problem is redistributing verbatim Yahoo-sourced transcripts, worse where they sit under the CC0 dedication in `proposals/` and `gold/`. The defeatbeta ODC-BY tag probably cannot authorise that, and the Zenodo/CFF CC0 metadata compounds it. Before publication, I would:
  1. Convert the earnings corpus and its quote tables to ids, offsets and hashes.
  2. Fix the licence metadata.
  3. Hold every deck rate until the gold set is coded, or recaption the rates as witness agreement.
  4. Report the risk trend per AI window.
</agent-message>

The paper's seal can't be checked because `seal.py` was never committed. The committed DECK.md fails its own `--check` and cites chart scripts and figures that aren't in the repo. The underlying engineering does work: the edition build reproduced every chart byte-for-byte from committed files, the DAG and attestation checks pass, and the bulk sources are pinned by hash with fetch instructions. The fixes, in priority order:
  1. Publish Praxis and pin its commit, for example in `dag.yml` or a `PRAXIS_REV` file, and name it in README.md.
  2. Commit `seal.py`, the README seal section, `sources/`, the deck outputs and `docs/figures/`, or remove the references to them.
  3. Make `--check` compare against `edition_of`.
  4. Pin the remaining `>=` dependencies.
  5. Make the missing-bulk `verify` paths fail cleanly instead of crashing.

  **M1. The numeral lint checks against one global set of allowed values, not the value each numeral is bound to** (`allowed()` `:152-156`). Confirmed by execution.
  - Any numeral matching any registered value passes in chart text or DECK.md: 46 of the integers 0–100 and 65 one-decimal values do.
  - The minus sign is dropped, so "−0.6%" becomes the token "0.6" and passes.
  - Spelled-out numbers ("twelve", "a third"), ½, ² and Roman numerals give no tokens at all.
  - Unicode digits are caught. Hand-typed chart strings in `build_edition.py` are checked only against this union.
  
  **M2. Some channels are never checked** (by reading, plus regex tests):
  - HTML comments are stripped before both checks (`:490,:504`). Marp turns non-directive comments into presenter notes in the pptx, so they ship unchecked.
  - Image alt text is stripped (`:491,:505`). Front matter, including the title, is excluded.
  - The SVG check reads only `el.text` of text/title/desc elements (`:192`). Text in `<tspan>`, tail text and `aria-label` escape it.
  - Matplotlib tick labels are exempt (`:171-182`). That covers barh category labels and the "(to 11-04)" as-of date on the year axis, which is data.
  - `STATIC_TOKENS` "500" is allowed everywhere, not only inside "S&P 500".
  
  **M3. Provenance and quotes are not verified.**
  - `Registry.put` (display.py:117-127) accepts any literal text with free-text source/how strings.
  - `q_33k` "33,000+" is checked only as a 7-character substring of the paper. The paper's own table (1afb042, line ~1092) gives the claim as ">33,000 critical- and high-severity flaws" and marks it UNVERIFIED, so the deck quotes the paper's paraphrase as if it were the claim.
  - The quote check matches straight double quotes only (`:495`). Curly quotes, single quotes and blockquotes evade it (confirmed).
  - Keys that copy another value (`ds_exec_safety`, `ec_pct_witness_last`) are written to STAMP without n/d.
  
  **M4. The CVE footnote's baseline date is the earnings-call as-of date** (`:477`), not the CVE baseline. Both are 2026-11-04 today, by coincidence. If either source is refreshed on its own, the footnote mislabels silently.
  
  **M5. The dialogue-space windows are hand-typed** (`v02_map.py:41-50`).
  - Executives use '2025-11-04' to '2026-11-04', HN uses 2025-09 to 2026-08, and Wikipedia has no date window.
  - None of these is tied to as-of. The build checks the question table's header against as-of (`:341-343`) but never checks the dialogue table.
  - The caption compares sources measured over different windows without saying so. `hn_report.py:155` also hand-types "12 months to 2026-11-04".
  
  **M6. Two captions overstate.**
  - "Cyber dominates HN's AI-security set (85%)" is close to true by definition: that set is selected on security terms (`hn_extract.py:19`). Likewise, the Wikipedia set is articles chosen because they are about AI risk.
  - "How AI sells (21.2%) far more than how its risk is controlled (0.8%)" sets the largest commercial intent against the smallest oversight one. The group totals are 32.2% commercial vs 5.7% oversight (529 and 94 of 1645), about 5.6x rather than about 26x.
  
  ### Low
  - **L1.** `display.score` says it rounds half up from the score's shortest decimal form. But `hn_report.py:136` casts the FLOAT32 score to DOUBLE first, so it rounds the double instead. In a sampled sweep 7 values differed; for example float32 0.38595 shows as 0.3859, not 0.3860. Confirmed.
  - **L2.** `hn_report.py:171-172` prints "0.0"/"0" when the denominator is 0, showing an undefined value as zero.
  - **L3.** "Any AI system on 924" includes credits that only use a generic AI term. The chart legend says "or term"; the caption does not.
  - **L4.** "Every number … read from a committed table" is not quite true: 12, 0.5 and 33,000+ are constants or a quote.
  - **L5.** The year series starts at 2013, but calls.tsv covers 2005–2012. I recomputed those years: all 0.0%, so "at most 0.6% in any year before 2023" still holds.
  
  ## Recompute (my own duckdb code over the raw parquet/TSV)
  | Value | Deck | Recomputed | Result |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | ec_nli_2026 | 12.2% | 214/1760 = 12.2 | match |
  | ec_pre2023_max | 0.6% | 10/1784 (2017) | match |
  | ec_ttm_risk_firms | 137 | 137 (issuer-only 79) | match; caption wrong (H1) |
  | top 3 by firms | 40/31/26 | 40/31/26 (issuer-only 20/14/14) | match |
  | q_revenue / q_controls | 21.2 / 0.8 | 348/1645, 13/1645 | match |
  | ds_exec_safety | 10% | 18/179, from raw scores | match |
  | ds_hn_safety / ds_wp_safety / ds_sec_cyber | 39 / 61 / 85 | 2466/6282, 504/833, 2640/3097 | match (arithmetic from the table) |
  | CVE Claude / any AI | 370 / 924 | 370 / 924; Claude ≤ AI in every month | match |
  
  Denominators: the questions header n (1645) equals all scored AI questions in the window. No duplicate-class or stub calls leak into the 12-month windows or the questions (0 found). The 12-month window boundaries match: exclusive at as-of minus 12 months, inclusive at as-of. October 2026 is a complete month for CVEs (the baseline is from 2026-11-04 01:00Z).
  
  ## Verdict
  Arithmetic and rounding are sound. Every headline number I recomputed matches exactly, and no float reaches DECK.md through display.py's formatters. The weak points are the checks around the numbers and what the captions claim.
  
  The numeral lint only proves a numeral appears somewhere in the registry, not that it is the right value. Comments, alt text, front matter, tick labels and tspan/attribute text are not checked at all. The quote check covers straight double quotes only, and provenance strings are not verified. The freshness `--check` is unsatisfiable after commit.
  
  The most serious problem is H1, a caption error rather than a control failure: "137 firms raised AI as a risk" is 79 if only company speakers count. M6 adds two framings that are close to true by construction or chosen to look large. Fix H1, H2 and M6 before the deck is published as tightly controlled.
  
  Scratch scripts: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-srvo-dev-anthropic-pbc/c1e11076-829e-4b02-9641-6b48b095d475/scratchpad/integrity/recompute.py, /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-srvo-dev-anthropic-pbc/c1e11076-829e-4b02-9641-6b48b095d475/scratchpad/integrity/lint.py, /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-srvo-dev-anthropic-pbc/c1e11076-829e-4b02-9641-6b48b095d475/scratchpad/integrity/f32.py
</agent-message>

  - **Why the wrapper:** it is exactly `./praxis report from-md edition/REPORT.md --template research --publisher praxis` with one addition. from-md runs Typst with its root at `praxis/report_writer`, and Typst cannot read files outside that root.   **Outline (Praxis research order):**
  1. **Bottom line:** an excerpt of the paper's Verdict 1 ("legally real and legally toothless"), then a human-written synthesis of what the outward data adds and does not.
  2. **Decision-relevant facts:** at most 10 rows picked from the claims: Verdicts 1–4, the 5–7% revenue from policy violators, the $895.4B commitments, and C0004 (analyst intent) and C0007 (CVE credits).
  3. **What could change the conclusion:** built from `falsifier-for` links. It lists each paper falsifier (§6 pillar falsifiers, §8 (1)–(6)), the data that tests it and the result. For example, §8 (1) "CVE-level counts" is tested by C0007: not met, not refuted. Then the data caveats (no gold set, draft codebook, survivorship).
  4. **Analysis:** one subsection per paper verdict. The paper's claims come first as excerpts with their status, then the data findings linked to them, then connecting prose for Sloane to write. The existing charts stay where they are.
  5. **Methods and limitations:** the Descartes quote audit (60 checked, 51 byte-exact), the witnesses, and one line per corpus "does not answer" section with a link.
  6. **Claims index:** paper and data claims in one id space, about 55 entries.
  7. **Sources index:** the paper at its pinned commit, the filings as the paper cites them, and the `dag.yml` steps.
  8. **Revision and review record:** counts of reviewed and unreviewed text, dataset and paper signature status, and a link to `review-2026-11-04.md`.
  
  **Linking model:**
  - **Ids that exist today:** the paper has GOV-1…GOV-10, the BERT spec has H1–H5, and the risk-governance index has rows 1–8. Appendix C, the verdicts and the falsifiers have no ids. Descartes ids are `blake3:` content ids, and the 234 Descartes rows are not in this repo. The report's own C-ids are currently assigned by position in `build_edition.CLAIMS`, so reordering renumbers them.
  - **One id space:** a committed `edition/claims.tsv` with columns `id, key, kind, statement_ref, status, aliases, upstream, review`. Ids are assigned once and never reused, and C0001–C0008 keep their numbers. Paper ids go in `aliases`; the Descartes id goes in `upstream` once Praxis question Q21 is settled.
  - **Claim → source:** one of three forms: a dag step URI plus file and BLAKE3 from `dag.lock.tsv`; `git:<commit>:<path>#char=a,b` plus the slice's BLAKE3 for paper and repo text; or an external URL marked "no captured digest".
  - **Claim → claim:** `edition/relations.tsv` with `supports`, `contradicts`, `qualifies` and `falsifier-for`. Every link is an inference with a review state. The build fails on a cycle, an unknown id, or a data claim linked to nothing.
  - **Prose:** paper text comes in as excerpts from `edition/excerpts.tsv` (commit, path, offsets, BLAKE3). The build re-slices each one from `git show` and fails on a hash mismatch, which generalises today's `QUOTES`. An excerpt's numbers are shown as the paper's figures with the paper's status, never as computed values. New prose is bound templates under the existing controls.
  - **Machine-readable:** the build writes `edition/claims.csv` in the Praxis register format, which feeds the Praxis report writer's appendix of claims needing attention. It also writes `edition/claims.jsonld`, using the Praxis `render/semantics.py` vocabularies (schema, dcterms, prov, dcat) plus `schema:Claim`, CiTO predicates and one local `falsifierFor` term. Praxis's emitter has no claim nodes today, so that extension is proposed upstream, not edited in Praxis.
  - **Marking unreviewed text:** each prose block's BLAKE3 is checked against `edition/review.tsv`, which only Sloane writes. A block with no matching hash renders under an "Unreviewed draft" label, and a `--release` flag refuses to build while any remain.
  
  **Open decisions for Sloane (my recommendation in brackets):**
  1. Which paper commit to pin: v0 `1afb042` or a later committed revision. [v0; moving the pin later is one commit and every excerpt re-verifies]
  2. Claim statements as verbatim one-sentence excerpts, or rewritten summaries. [verbatim]
  3. Id scheme: C-ids with paper ids as aliases, paper ids as primary, or Descartes ids as primary. [C-ids with aliases]
  4. Praxis Q21: every binding becomes a Descartes claim, only quotes from outside documents do, or the two stay separate. [only outside quotes; the excerpts map directly onto Descartes rows]
  5. Publisher and disclosure. Paper v0 says "From: The Praxis project (Ethical Capital)" and carries an investment-adviser publication note, but the report publishes as `praxis`. Options: carry the note verbatim, drop it and keep §8's standing pass, or wait for the Ethical Capital publisher. [carry it verbatim after a compliance review, because the GOV recommendations and investor-directed text would now ship in the report]
  6. Relation vocabulary: CiTO plus one local term, or local terms only. [CiTO plus one local term]
  7. Claims and sources indexes inline in REPORT.md, or as companion pages. [inline unless the PDF passes 25 pages]
  8. Include the risk-governance index's 8 rows as inference claims, or leave them out because its content hash is unsealed. [include them, marked unsealed]

Okay, so the buzzsaw argumentation is really, there's this writing concept from South Park where they often a lot of things happen where it's like, or a lot of people will write, we'll tell a story, it's like this happened, and then this happened and then this happened. Right? South Park always goes, this happened because this happened, because this happened, because this happened, right? So ideally, what we do is have a causality matrix and be sort of sitting there and going, okay, here's what kicked it off, and then here's how it all rolls. And we'd be as we rework it and add evidence, we can pull stuff in.

Okay, well, this seems like we need to be better at editorial stuff, right? Like, if we're mistranslating something into a quote, or if we're quoting something and losing the context, that's a big problem. So we need to kind of harden that. And then also, We really need to be able to kick off a review, like a search for research on that sort of stuff. Like, is there no source in the entire research corpus? If so, then we need to kind of identify the question, figure it out, you know, whatever. The idea is to do reviews at the atomic level so that as we build up, we can be increasingly on an increasingly firm foundation and also hold it out to others who are not experts that they can trace the whole thing

  **Other failure causes**
  - Punctuation or spelling that isn't in the source: Q006 (a hyphen in "wholly-owned"), Q007 and Q054 (trailing commas), Q067 (a final period).
  - Paraphrase inside quotation marks: Q011, Q039, Q044, Q084, Q090.
  - One word or short phrase that occurs many times: Q015, Q043, Q048, Q079.
  
  **The 5 most serious context problems**
  1. **Q067:** the paper says the S-1 "generalizes without attributing" misalignment. But the S-1 risk factors say these behaviours "have been observed both in our models and elsewhere in the industry". They also report that four of Anthropic's own models gained unauthorized access to real third-party systems. The paper's point is contradicted by its own source.
  2. **Q015:** the paper glosses "pacing the frontier" as industry-wide standards so that capabilities don't outrun safety. It drops the source's scope, "primarily related to the use of AI to accelerate AI development".
  3. **Q043 (also at the §6.2 use of Q044):** the paper says the S-1 frames labour risk as perception of displacement. That is one risk factor, about reputation. Another treats displacement "whether actual or perceived" and contemplates actions "to ameliorate the impact of such displacement".
  4. **Q079:** the paper's question about a founder no longer "continuing", and its "transition when <3 continuing founders (450 days)", leave out two things. The S-1's "Continuing Founders" includes successor members admitted until the tenth anniversary of the offering. The transition starts only after fewer than three for 90 consecutive days.
  5. **Q048 and Q013 (fidelity slips I marked requote):**
     - Q048: the source says the actor "targeted" at least 17 organizations and attempted extortion; the paper writes "extortion ≥17 orgs".
     - Q013: dropping "We believe that" turns a stated belief into something the S-1 "says".
     - Q039, a smaller point: "not directly reflective of the ongoing operating performance" is the S-1's reason for all its non-GAAP exclusions, not a label it gives the Bartz settlement alone.

  - **No page, section or offset column.** Only 3 rows mention a page, and only in statement prose. The `§` in statements is the paper's section, not the source's. Every value is computable, as shown above.
  - **No digest of the text checked against.** `path` names a gitignored file. The row records the prompt digest but not the evidence digest.
  
  ## steps.tsv and review.tsv
  - **The commit column says "see MANIFEST.tsv history" or "see derived/recipes.tsv history"** where git already gives hashes (2e49f65, 4309137, 70a8866, 1b02d1f). S15 says "the commit that adds this row".
  - **review.tsv `source` carries location as free prose** ("(Report, Foreword by Daniela Amodei)"), with no page field.
  
  ## docs/emissions.tsv
  - **Records only date, file, sha256, bytes, built_from and destination.** It leaves out the Descartes commit and binary SHA, the Praxis commit (`a8a0fe581c0d`, from git), Typst 0.15.1, page count (28) and the dirty flag. The appendix says "full values are in the records", but emissions.tsv does not hold them.
  
  ## comments/anthropic-contrapunctus.tsv and scripts/pdf_comments.py
  - **Annotation time and position are dropped.** pymupdf exposes `modDate` (e.g. `D:20261104183722-07'00'`) and `rect`. The ledger keeps only page, kind, author, a 240-character anchor and the content, and `received` is a typed argument. *Matters:* it orders comments and pins them inside the page.
  
  ## Emitted PDF (anthropic-pbc-working-paper-1abaa3d.pdf)
  - **The provenance line has no software versions.** Appendix F names the paper commit, the Descartes commit and binary SHA. It leaves out the Praxis commit, Typst, poppler, Python 3.15.2, uv 0.13.0 and the build host.
  - **The front page has no commit or version.** It shows only "v0" and "2026-11-04". This is C5, still open.
  - **The Info dictionary is thin.** Title is "Anthropic PBC", not the paper's title. No Subject, Keywords or Producer, and nothing carries the commit. CreateDate is set to `2026-11-04T00:00:00Z`, which `pdfinfo` shows as "Tue Nov 3 17:00 MST": the document reads as built the day before.
  - **Accessibility:** Tagged yes, language `en` (both /Lang and XMP), title set. Of 2 `/S /Figure` structure elements, 1 has no `/Alt`. I did not verify which figure lacks it (it may be a logo).
  - **Fonts are embedded and subset** (Libertinus, DejaVu Mono) but unrecorded.
  
  ## UNVERIFIED
  - Whether prompts/ record the model's sampling parameters. I did not inspect them.
  - index.html metadata.
  
  ## Five gaps to close first
  1. Page and offset for each claim: drop `-nopgbrk`, or emit a page map, and use the S-1 "N Table of Contents" footers.
  2. SHA-256 in MANIFEST.tsv, plus the derived-text digest on each claim row.

  4. EDGAR accession, form or exhibit type, and filing date as manifest columns.
  5. A full build stamp (Praxis commit, Typst, poppler, Python and uv, Descartes) on the front page, in the PDF Info/XMP, and in emissions.tsv. Also fix the midnight-UTC CreateDate.

  ## Pricing benchmarks for the evidence commons: research findings (read-only, no files changed)
  
  **Short answer:** I found a usable market-size series and several licensing prices. I found **no published share of vendor revenue or data that comes from nonprofit or public-interest sources**. That number has not been published anywhere I could reach.
  
  ### 1. Annual spending on ESG data (the vendors' term)
  
  Opimas is the only firm that publishes a running series. Its full reports are paywalled; I checked only the free summaries and press coverage.
  
  | Year | Estimate | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | 2018 | $505M | Opimas tweet, Jan 2019 (x.com/OpimasLLC/status/1083724792132444160). Covers "ESG content and indices". Primary but thin. |
  | 2019 | $617M | Opimas, 9 Mar 2020, opimas.com/research/547/detail/. Primary. Data growing about 20% a year, indices about 35%. Europe is about 60% of spending, asset managers 59%. |
  | 2020 (forecast) | $745M | Opimas figure quoted in the European Commission (EC) / SustainAbility "Study on Sustainability-Related Ratings, Data and Research", Jan 2021 (op.europa.eu/en/publication-detail/-/publication/d7d85036-509c-11eb-b59f-01aa75ed71a1). It splits about $525M "ESG content" from about $240M indices, which does not quite add up to $745M. Secondary. |
  | 2021 | Over $1B | Opimas, 19 Apr 2022, opimas.com/research/742/detail/. Primary summary. |
  | 2023 | $1.9B | Reuters, 11 Sep 2024, citing Opimas. Press report. MSCI held 25% of this. |
  | 2024 (forecast) | Over $2B, or $2.1B | Opimas, 11 Mar 2024, opimas.com/research/982/detail/ (primary, says over $2B). The $2.1B figure (up 14%) comes from NeoXam, 27 Mar 2024, which is secondary. |
  | 2025 (forecast) | $2.2B, up 11% | Responsible Investor, 15 Oct 2025, paywalled, citing Opimas's report of 14 Oct 2025 (opimas.com/research/1045/detail/). Opimas forecasts 8% growth in 2026 and 6% after. |
  
  - **Method:** Opimas says it uses "publicly available information, interviews, as well as companies' actual or estimated revenues". It counts ratings, raw data and analytics together, plus index licensing. In 2021, research and analytics were about 70% and indices about 30% (over $300M). It does **not** include corporate reporting software.
  - **Concentration:** in 2021 the top three vendors (MSCI, ISS ESG, Sustainalytics) held about 60%.
  - **Disagreements:**
    - A "$5B by 2025" forecast circulates, for example in Capco's Journal. I could not find who made it, so it is unverified.
    - Commercial market-research firms publish much higher figures with no stated method. Mordor Intelligence says $12.69B for "ESG rating services" in 2026. I would not use these.
    - I found no market-size estimates from Burgiss, Bloomberg Intelligence or Substantive Research. Substantive Research publishes vendor price inflation only: 18% in 2023, 5.2% in 2024, 10% in 2025.
  - **Check from the vendor side:** MSCI's Sustainability and Climate segment had annualised recurring revenue of $384.8M at 30 Sep 2026 (MSCI Q3 2026 release, ir.msci.com, 20 Oct 2026, primary).
  
  ### 2. How much vendor data comes from nonprofit sources
  
  **I found no quantified share.** The qualitative evidence:
  
  - **MSCI** names among its inputs "macro data at segment or geographic level from academic, government and NGO datasets", alongside company disclosures and media, NGO and other stakeholder sources. The EC 2021 study above reproduces this. MSCI gives no weights.
  - **RepRisk** screens "media, government and regulatory publications, NGOs, think tanks…" and takes 100% of its revenue from subscribers (RepRisk's disclosures under EU Regulation 2024/3005, reprisk.com, Oct 2026, primary). It publishes no breakdown by source type.
  - **CDP** is itself on Opimas's list of ESG data vendors (Opimas, 2020). CDP sells its company-response datasets under licence and charges commercial users. In its words: "A subsidized cost-based fee may apply" for non-commercial users (cdp.net/en/data-licenses, primary).
  - **Berg, Kölbel and Rigobon, "Aggregate Confusion"** (Review of Finance, 2022), cited from memory and not re-fetched. It breaks down why ratings disagree: measurement differences 56%, scope 38%, weighting 6%. It does not trace where the inputs come from.
  
  ### 3. Nonprofits that charge commercial users
  
  - **CDP** (year to 31 Mar 2025 accounts, primary, downloads.ctfassets.net/…/Trustees_Report_2025.pdf):
    - Total income £60.46M.
    - Charitable trading of £10.62M, a figure that bundles investor memberships, data sales and disclosure fees. **Data sales are not reported on their own.**
    - Other trading £13.95M and grants £24.10M.
  - **CDP and Permira:** on 11 Jun 2026 CDP announced it would split in two, with Permira investing in a for-profit arm (cdp.net press release and permira.com, primary). A Substack piece by Hugh Wheelan (29 Jul 2026) reports from unnamed sources that Permira will pay about £100M to the CDP Foundation, which keeps a minority stake. That figure is unverified. **This bears directly on the commons argument.**
  - **Wikimedia Enterprise:** revenue was $3.2M in FY2022-23, $3.4M in FY2023-24 and $8.297M in FY2024-25. The last figure is in the audited FY2024-25 statements (wikimediafoundation.org annual report, and diff.wikimedia.org, 24 Nov 2025, both primary). The FY2025-26 plan targets five AI contracts.
  - **OpenCorporates:** offers free share-alike access for public-benefit users and paid annual plans for everyone else (opencorporates.com terms). **I found no revenue figure.**
  - **OpenStreetMap (ODbL):** I found no direct data-licensing revenue.
  
  ### 4. Prices paid for AI training data
  
  - **Reddit:** agreements signed in January 2024 have "aggregate contract value of $203.0 million and terms ranging from two to three years" (Reddit S-1, sec.gov, Feb 2024, primary). The Google deal is about $60M a year (Reuters, 21 Feb 2024, from sources; press report). Data-licensing revenue was $43M in Q2 2026, about 5% of total revenue (Crypto Briefing, 17 Aug 2026, citing The Verge; secondary).
  - **News Corp and OpenAI:** more than $250M over five years, including credits (WSJ, 22 May 2024, from sources; paywalled press report).
  - **News Corp and Meta:** up to $50M a year (WSJ, 4 Mar 2026, headline only; paywalled).
  
  ### The 3 most reliable figures
  
  1. Reddit's $203.0M in aggregate data-licensing contracts over 2–3 years, from its SEC filing.
  2. Wikimedia Enterprise revenue of $8.30M in FY2024-25, from audited statements.
  3. CDP's FY2024-25 income of £60.46M, of which £10.62M is trading income that includes data sales, from audited accounts. The Opimas series (about $2.2B in 2025) is the best market-size figure, but its method is proprietary and the full reports are paywalled.
  
  ### What computing "the share of vendor spend attributable to nonprofit sources" would need
  
  - **Revenue by product line for each vendor.** Opimas has this but keeps it behind its paywall.
  - **Which inputs feed each product,** for example how many rating indicators draw on CDP responses, NGO reports or government registries. This could be worked out from methodology documents.
  - **Weights or value attribution for those inputs.** Vendors do not publish these, though the EU ESG ratings regulation's transparency rules may surface some.
  - **Upstream licence fees vendors actually pay,** such as CDP's data-sales line, which is not broken out.
  - **A defensible rule for allocating value** between raw inputs and the vendor's own analysis.
</agent-message>

  ## External topologies: what each body publishes, and whether its terms let us fetch it
  
  Read-only research; no files were written. Pages were fetched 2026-11-04/05 with TinyFish, curl HEAD requests and the public Wayback CDX API. I did not log in anywhere or send any personal identifiers.
  
  ### Terms of use: bodies whose terms block our fetch rule
  | Body | Clause (verbatim) | Source | Verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | **CFP Board** | Prohibited Conduct includes "(10) Use of the CFP Board Content for any machine learning and/or artificial intelligence purposes, including to train artificial intelligence models."   | **GARP (FRM, SCR, RAI)** | §7: "You may not use any robot, spider, crawler, scraper, data-mining tool … to access, extract, harvest, download, copy, or index any portion of the Site" and no use "to feed, seed, or prompt any generative AI systems, or to create derivative datasets". robots.txt is incorporated into the terms.   | **Investments & Wealth Institute (CIMA)** | "use of data mining, robots, or similar data-gathering and extraction tools is prohibited. Neither this site nor any portion … may be reproduced, duplicated, copied…" | https://investmentsandwealth.org/privacy-policy (its "Conditions of Use", updated January 15, 2025) | **Automated extraction barred. Treat as do-not-fetch.** |
  | **IFRS Foundation (ISSB/SASB taxonomies)** | "Reproduction and use rights are strictly limited. to personal non-commercial use, such as corporate disclosure. Any other use, such as—but not limited to—reporting software, investment analysis, data services and product development is not permitted without written consent." The terms define "AI Model". The taxonomies are "free of charge for non-commercial purposes … Any other use … requires a licence". | https://www.ifrs.org/legal/terms-and-conditions/ ; https://www.ifrs.org/digital-financial-reporting/ | **Investment-analysis use is named as barred. Do not fetch without a licence.** I could not extract an operative AI clause, only the definition (UNVERIFIED). |
  | **CAIA Association** | "You may view, download and print the Content for your personal, non-commercial use … the use or posting of the Content on any other website or in a network computer environment for any purpose are expressly prohibited." No AI clause found. Updated October 1, 2024. | https://caia.org/terms-of-use/ | No AI bar, but **committing or storing copies in a public repo is barred**. |
  | **CFA Institute** | "authorizes you to view and download a single copy … solely for your personal, noncommercial use … may not … reproduce, display … or otherwise use the material in any way for any public or commercial purpose." No AI or scraping clause found in the terms. The AI Usage Statement (17 Nov 2025) covers only CFA's own AI use. | https://www.cfainstitute.org/about/governance/policies/terms-conditions ; .../ai-usage-statement | No AI bar, but **public redistribution is barred**. |
  
  robots.txt at cfainstitute.org, caia.org, cfp.net, investmentsandwealth.org, ifrs.org and garp.org has no AI-crawler-specific blocks; each shows only `User-agent: *`.
  
  **Decision for Sloane:** CFA and CAIA could be pinned by hash only: URL, bytes and SHA-256, with no bytes or extracted text committed. Whether that fits "single copy, personal, noncommercial" is an editorial or legal call I can't make.
  
  ### 1. R world (all open)
  - **CRAN package metadata.** Verified live by HEAD request on 2026-11-05 and regenerated daily:
    - `src/contrib/PACKAGES` (7.4 MB, DCF text)
    - `src/contrib/PACKAGES.rds` (1.3 MB)
    - `web/packages/packages.rds` (12.7 MB, the full `CRAN_package_db`)
    - `src/contrib/Meta/archive.rds` (5.3 MB, the history of every archived version)
    - Granularity is the package, with Depends, Imports, Suggests and LinkingTo edges, which give a deterministic dependency graph.
    - History: `archive.rds` covers package versions. CRAN does not version the PACKAGES file itself, so pin snapshots.
    - Terms: CRAN policy requires each package licence to "give the right for CRAN to distribute the package in perpetuity" (https://cran.r-project.org/web/packages/policies.html). I found no terms clause covering the metadata itself (UNVERIFIED).
  - **CRAN Task Views.** 50 views, listed at https://cran.r-project.org/web/views/. Each view is its own git repo under github.com/cran-task-views, with a Markdown source such as https://raw.githubusercontent.com/cran-task-views/Finance/main/Finance.md (22 KB).
    - Finance has 338 repo commits, the oldest dated **2007-06-08**, so the old SVN history was imported.
    - Commits touching `Econometrics.md` start 2021-09-17 and `OfficialStatistics.md` 2021-12-29, when the views were converted to Markdown. Earlier history under the old `.ctv` filenames is UNVERIFIED.
    - Granularity is the package list per view, which can be diffed commit by commit. The initiative is described in Zeileis et al. (2025), R Journal, doi:10.32614/RJ-2025-011.
  - **R-universe.** Live JSON APIs: https://r-universe.dev/api/universes (2.9 MB) and https://ropensci.r-universe.dev/api/packages (8.5 MB, rOpenSci's package registry). The old `ropensci/roregistry` registry.json URLs return 404. Terms UNVERIFIED.
  - **Bioconductor.** https://bioconductor.org/packages/release/bioc/VIEWS (5.3 MB, DCF format with biocViews ontology terms). Only relevant if we want a second package ontology.
  
  ### 2. Open-data world (top 3)
  - **Our World in Data ETL.** The DAG is YAML at https://raw.githubusercontent.com/owid/etl/master/dag/main.yml (30 KB). The repo is MIT-licensed (GitHub API) and uses the same snapshot://, data:// URI scheme as our `dag.yml`, so it joins most naturally with our own work. https://catalog.ourworldindata.org/catalog.meta.json answers. OWID data licence (CC BY) not re-checked: UNVERIFIED.
  - **data.gov.** The CKAN catalog was "replaced … in 2025" by a custom Flask app (https://github.com/GSA/data.gov/wiki/catalog.data.gov). The old `/api/3/action/` endpoint now returns 404. The current schema is DCAT-US v3.0 (https://resources.data.gov/resources/dcat-us3/). The new API endpoint is UNVERIFIED.

  ## Inventory: sources that state what humanity needs (checked 2026-11-04, public web only via TinyFish, no logins, no identifiers sent)
  
  I mark anything I did not confirm on a primary page as UNVERIFIED. **I found no source whose terms explicitly bar AI ingestion.** The real limits are licences that forbid derivatives or redistribution, which I flag below.
  
  ### A. Negotiated or adopted frameworks (they speak through states)
  - **SDGs (2030 Agenda).** 17 goals and 169 targets. The indicator framework was adopted in A/RES/71/313 and has been refined every year through the 57th Statistical Commission (March 2026). It now has **234 unique indicators (251 listed, 13 repeated)** (unstats.un.org/sdgs/indicators/indicators-list/). Data comes from the UNSD SDG API v5 (unstats.un.org/sdgs/UNSDGAPIV5/swagger/), which has an `SDGArchiveData` schema, so historical versions exist. Licence: unstats has no terms page of its own (UNVERIFIED). The general un.org terms allow only "personal, non-commercial use, without any right to resell or redistribute them or to compile or create derivative works therefrom." UNdata (data.un.org, now hosting the UN System Data Commons) instead says data "may be copied freely, duplicated and further distributed provided that UNdata is cited." **Pin SDG data through UNdata where it is available.** Join: target codes such as "3.4" are a standard codelist (IATI uses one), so any SDG claim a company makes joins on the code.
  - **UDHR and ICESCR.** ICESCR has 173 of 193 member states (OHCHR CESCR page). Ratification data: the UN Treaty Collection pages, and a CSV mirror at github.com/world-politics-datalab/humanrights-treaties-ratification-status (licence UNVERIFIED). The UDHR vote of 48-0-8 is UNVERIFIED. The **Danish Institute for Human Rights SDG–Human Rights Data Explorer** (sdgdata.humanrights.dk) links UN human-rights recommendations to SDG targets and covers more than 50 instruments. That gives a deterministic bridge from rights to targets. Its licence is UNVERIFIED.
  - **UNDRIP.** Adopted 13 Sep 2007 by 144 votes to 4 (Australia, Canada, New Zealand, US), with 11 abstentions (OHCHR, UN DESA). It is text only, with numbered articles.
  - **AU Agenda 2063.** This is Africa's own framework, launched with the 2013 Solemn Declaration and implemented through ten-year plans (au.int). AU membership of 55 is UNVERIFIED, and I did not find a machine-readable indicator set (UNVERIFIED).
  - **Paris Agreement.** It has 194 Parties as of 27 Jan 2026, after the US withdrawal took effect (unfccc.int). It has a dated party list. Join: temperature goal and NDC targets set against company emissions disclosures.
  - **Kunming-Montreal GBF.** It has 4 goals for 2050 and 23 targets for 2030, plus a monitoring framework, in decision 15/4 (cbd.int/gbf). The count of 196 CBD parties is UNVERIFIED (the US is not a party).
  - **ILO conventions.** Ratifications are in NORMLEX. Coverage and licence are UNVERIFIED.
  - **Global Digital Compact.** "Negotiated by 193 Member States" (un.org/global-digital-compact). Its paragraphs are numbered, and it is the closest AI-specific text adopted by states.
  - **UN HLAB-AI, "Governing AI for Humanity"** (Sept 2024). PDF at un.org/sites/un2.un.org/files/governing_ai_for_humanity_final_report_en.pdf. It is an expert body, not adopted by states. Its numbered recommendations could join to company governance disclosures. The PDF falls under un.org's no-derivatives terms, but quoting it with a hash pin is fine.
  
  ### B. Measured conditions
  - **UNDP HDR (HDI, IHDI, GII, PHDI) and the global MPI.** Licence verified: "Creative Commons Attribution 3.0 IGO … for any purpose, even commercially" (hdr.undp.org/terms-use). There is a full composite time series plus an API (hdrdata.org, needs a key). The **MPI 2025** covers 109 countries and 6.3 billion people, with 1,359 subnational regions. OPHI Data Table 8 holds every release since 2010, so versions exist. OPHI's own data-table licence is UNVERIFIED (its do-files are under the "Oxford Academic license"). This is the most rigorous count of who lacks what.
  - **World Bank WDI.** Licence verified: CC BY 4.0 by default. Its microdata uses separate research licences (datacatalog.worldbank.org/public-licenses).
  - **WHO GHO.** Licence verified as **restrictive**: use is allowed "for public health purposes". Any change beyond minimal styling needs "prior written authorization of WHO", and the data may not be used "in conjunction with the promotion of a commercial enterprise." FLAG: derived tables need care.
  - **Planetary boundaries (SRC, 2026 update).** The graphics are CC BY-NC-ND 3.0 (verified). The underlying values sit in the papers (Richardson 2023; Sakschewski & Caesar 2026), with licences UNVERIFIED. These are expert assertions, not people speaking.
  - **Doughnut (Fanning, O'Neill, Hickel, Roux 2022).** About 150 countries over time. Spreadsheets at goodlife.leeds.ac.uk/download-data/ (…SocialShortfallAndEcologicalOvershoot_SupplementaryData.xlsx). Licence unstated (UNVERIFIED). A revised 35-indicator version is in Nature 2025.
  - **Social Progress Index.** 171 countries in 2026. Licence verified: **no republishing of datasets and "No Derivative Works Without License"**, and the downloadable dataset costs USD 42. FLAG: not usable in a public rebuildable corpus.
  - **Our World in Data.** OWID's own processing is CC BY. Third-party data keeps its source licence, and Grapher code needs permission. Use it as a pointer and pin the upstream source.
  
  ### C. People speaking for themselves
  - **Afrobarometer.** Round 10 merged file covers 38 countries (2025). Since 1999 it has run more than 200 surveys with over 330,000 respondents, face to face in local languages, with n≥1,200 per country. African-led. Licence: "protected by copyright", citation required, free download. Redistribution terms UNVERIFIED. Geocoded data is restricted.
  - **Arab Barometer.** Free, but a contact form comes before download. FLAG: the form asks for personal details, which conflicts with our no-identifiers rule.
  - **Asian Barometer.** Wave 5 covers 15 countries, Wave 6 has 11 released (March 2026, including Australia), and the South Asian Barometer covers 5 countries. Licence verified as **restrictive**: application required, and users "must not copy… or otherwise supply any of the data… to any other individual", for academic or approved purposes only.
  - **Latinobarómetro.** About 20,000 interviews a year in 18 countries. Non-commercial academic use per a secondary source (Emory guide). Primary terms UNVERIFIED.
  - **LAPOP AmericasBarometer.** Free, but "Authors must not share data files." Replication has to go through links and code. That fits our pin-and-rebuild model.
  - **WVS and EVS.** The trend file runs 1981–2022 with 452 surveys in 115 countries. Free with registration under a "non-redistribution data use license." Every file has a DOI (doi:10.14281/18241.27). Join: Anthropic's own GlobalOpinionQA (HF `Anthropic/llm_global_opinions`, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0) is built from WVS and Pew items, which gives a direct item-level join to Anthropic's published work.
  - **Lloyd's Register Foundation World Risk Poll.** 570,475 interviews in 140 countries across the 2019, 2021, 2024 and 2026 reports (latest round: over 143,000 people). Fielded by Gallup on probability samples. Licence verified: "freely available to download and use (with attribution)." A versioning issue: a corrected file replaced the old one on 2 Sep 2026 and "the previous data file has been removed", so **we must keep our own saved bytes as the pin.** Join: the 2019 wave asked whether AI will "mostly help/harm" (41% vs 30%, per arXiv 2001.00081; UNVERIFIED against the primary source).
  - **Gallup World Poll.** More than 160 countries, paid microdata. Free subsets come through clients: the Global Preferences Survey (about 80,000 people in 76 countries; briq access terms UNVERIFIED), FAO Voices of the Hungry (FIES, SDG 2.1.2, 77 countries), Findex, the Wellcome Global Monitor, and Prindex.
  - **UN75 (2020).** 1.3 million respondents from 193 countries, in 64 languages, self-selected. Raw download at data.undp.org/insights/un75. It **includes free-text answers from private individuals**, so per AGENTS.md we store ids and hashes only. Licence UNVERIFIED.
  - **MY World 2015.** About 9.7 million votes on 16 priorities (ODI and WHO secondary sources), self-selected. A primary data URL and the MY World 2030 data are UNVERIFIED.
  - **World Bank "Voices of the Poor".** About 60,000 people, around 1999–2000. Size and data availability UNVERIFIED. It is qualitative and mostly in book form.
  - **CIP Global Dialogues.** About 1,000 participants per round from 63–80 countries in 7 languages, through Prolific and Remesh. CSV on Google Drive (376 MB) and HF `collective-intelligence-project/Global-AI-Dialogues`. **No licence stated, and the README is empty**, so the licence is UNVERIFIED and should be treated as all rights reserved until CIP confirms. CIP's own GRI paper (arXiv 2602.14835) scores its demographic fidelity at only 0.33–0.36, and it is online-only. Join: AI-specific items, and Anthropic was a "committed audience" for CIP's alignment assemblies.
  - **Anthropic Collective Constitutional AI.** About 1,000 **US-only** adults on Polis, screened for familiarity with generative AI. Processing code and data at github.com/saffronh/ccai (licence not seen, UNVERIFIED). Join: the public's principles can be compared directly with Anthropic's own constitution. Its scope is narrow: it is not "humanity".
  - **Indigenous Navigator.** Community-generated data, "by Indigenous Peoples, for Indigenous Peoples", monitoring UNDRIP rights. Explorable without an account. Bulk download and licence UNVERIFIED. Treat it under CARE principles: ask before ingesting.
  - Not checked: Meta/Stanford deliberative polls (UNVERIFIED).
  
  ### The five broadest, most legitimate, most computable sources
  1. **The SDG indicator framework, pinned through UNdata.** It has the widest state adoption (all 193), stable codes for joining, versioned annual refinements, and a redistributable route.
  2. **ICESCR/UDHR ratifications plus the DIHR SDG–Human Rights mapping.** These are needs stated as binding rights. Ratification counts are a computable measure of standing, and the mapping makes rights and SDGs one graph.
  3. **The UNDP HDR and global MPI.** CC BY 3.0 IGO, 109 countries and 1,359 regions, with a full release history. They measure who actually lacks what, built up from household surveys.
  4. **The Lloyd's Register World Risk Poll.** Openly licensed, probability samples in 140 countries, 570,000 people, and an AI item. It is the most computable source of people speaking for themselves, provided we keep our own copy.
  5. **The Global Barometer family plus WVS (Afrobarometer, Latinobarómetro/LAPOP, Arab, Asian).** Regionally led, face-to-face, in local languages, with decades of trend data, and WVS joins directly to Anthropic's GlobalOpinionQA. The cost is that most forbid redistribution, so we commit ids and hashes plus a fetch recipe, and a stranger must register to rebuild.
  
  CIP Global Dialogues is the necessary AI-specific complement. It is not in the five because it is not a probability sample and has no licence.
  
  ### Who does not get to speak
  - **States speak for people** in group A. The US has left Paris, CANZUS voted against UNDRIP in 2007, and the GDC and HLAB were drafted by elites.
  - **The offline and the unreachable.** Online panels (Global Dialogues, CCAI) exclude them. Even face-to-face surveys skip conflict zones, closed states and insecure areas (Afrobarometer says this itself).
  - **Children and young adolescents.** Most surveys sample adults or people aged 15 and over.
  - **Non-citizens, stateless people, migrants and the institutionalised.** They fall outside the sampling frames of national probability samples.
  - **Indigenous peoples.** National samples do not oversample them. Their own data source (Indigenous Navigator) carries sovereignty constraints, rightly.
  - **Pacific and Oceania.** Thin coverage: the Pacific appears only in HDI, World Risk Poll (WRP) and Asian Barometer (Australia).
  - **Future generations and non-humans.** They are represented only by expert assertion (planetary boundaries).
  - **Self-selection.** MY World and UN75 have huge numbers but are not representative.
  - **What people say about AI is the thinnest layer.** One WRP item, Global Dialogues, and a US-only CCAI.
  
  No files were written.
</agent-message>
