  # Design: the Rust lake hierarchy (organisation, deduplication, analysis)
  
  Read-only research. Dark was read only through `git show` and `git ls-tree`. Nothing was modified.
  
  **Sourcing notes.**
  - **C27–C30 are in no committed ref I could find.** `requirements.json` on origin/main, codex/dark-buildkite (stops at C18) and claude/conformance-topology (24 rows) does not have them. The wording below comes from the inference-cost doc and a session checkpoint (00627fb42):
    - C27: deterministic replay;
    - C28: simulation before consequential execution;
    - C29: friction reduction at each step;
    - C30: model inference declines per comparable successful outcome.
    All four are assessed "missing".
  - **I could not find the exclusions feeder in Dark refs.** The only place I found it is Praxis `resources/topologies/web-data/README.md`, which names it as the consumer of `permission-evidence.tsv` (rule R003).
  
  ## 1. Inventory
  
  | Layer | Exists (path) | Reuse vs replace |
  |---|---|---|
  | L0 bytes | Dark `tools/addams-catalog` (codex/dark-buildkite): Rust walk, magic sniff, SHA-256, byte-identical ledger, PII never hashed. It ran over the 223k-file tray in 3m52s. Descartes `src/` uses BLAKE3 `=1.8.7` `pure`, checked against the official vectors. topo `MANIFEST.tsv` already records both sha256 and blake3. | **Extend** addams-catalog (cheap). Do not write a second walker. |
  | L1 catalog | addams-catalog rules (`R-PII-QUARANTINE`…); `dark.storage.census` (R2 census); `catalog.addams_*` migration (not applied); Praxis `catalog/`. | Reuse. The Python sync stays. |
  | L2 dedup | Exact: addams-catalog `duplicates.jsonl`. Person resolution: `packages/python/dark/dedup` (blocking, LLM clusters, reversible writes). Near-dup: none built. | Build near-dup only. Person resolution stays Python. |
  | L3 extract | Descartes `derived/recipes.tsv` (`pdftotext`, `html-to-text static-v1`), `topo.py text`. | Reuse the recipes. Write no new PDF parser (producer contract). |
  | L4 index | Qdrant (frozen embeddings), ClickHouse with `s3()` over the R2 lake (`infra/k3s/analytics`). | Reuse. Add tantivy only for local full-text. |
  | L5 analysis | ClickHouse + Metabase on k3s5. | Reuse first. |
  | L6 checks | Descartes `check`/`coverage`; topo `verify`/`gate`; `check_exclusion_sources.py`; continuous-verification draft. | Reuse unchanged. |
  | L7 proposals | `dark.dedup` completer, Bifrost, `triage.auto_spam_decisions`. | Reuse, gated. |
  | Run/observe | `tools/addams-api` (ledger, leases, fd-3 events, `cost_ceiling_usd`, `output_digest`); KEDA ScaledJobs `infra/k3s/jobs`; `critical-crons` + `cron_heartbeat_wrapper.py` + `catalog.pipeline_expectations`. | Reuse. No new scheduler. |
  | Emit | `packages/python/dark/jobs/events.py`; Praxis `distribution/process_events.py`; `planning.runs/events` (PRIMITIVES.md). | Reuse. |
  
  ## 2. The hierarchy
  
  Every step writes one **step receipt**, a canonical JSON file with these fields:
  - `actor`;
  - `tool` plus git commit and `Cargo.lock` digest;
  - `rule_ids`;
  - `params_digest`;
  - `inputs[]` and `outputs[]`, each as `{blake3, sha256, bytes}`.
  
  No clock, host or random id appears in the receipt. Wall time lives only in the Addams ledger rows. The receipt's digest is the Addams `result.output_digest`, and `process_events.emit` mirrors it to Praxis.
  
  | L | Contract | Determinism | Crates (licence, maturity) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | L0 bytes | in: paths or pins; out: manifest rows (blake3 primary, sha256 for catalog compatibility, one read pass) | byte-identical | `blake3` (CC0/Apache-2.0, mature; pin Descartes' version); `sha2` (MIT/Apache); `walkdir` (MIT/Unlicense) |
  | L1 catalog | in: manifest; out: `items.parquet`, each row with class, rule id and evidence. Unmatched rows are explicitly `unclassified`. | byte-identical | `arrow`, `parquet` (Apache-2.0, ASF, mature). Fixed row groups, zstd at a fixed level, rows sorted by digest. |
  | L2 dedup | exact groups by digest; near-dup by word-shingle MinHash (128 hashes, LSH 16×8), then exact Jaccard verification on every candidate. Pairs are **proposals**; nothing is merged automatically. | byte-identical (seeded `xxh3`) | `xxhash-rust` (BSL-1.0); `gaoya` (MIT, maturity UNVERIFIED) or ~100 lines in-house; `probminhash` (MIT/Apache, single maintainer) |
  | L3 extract | in: blob plus recipe; out: text and `segments.parquet` (blob, byte offsets, `needs_ocr` per page) | byte-identical if the recipe tool is pinned | none new (calls the Descartes recipes) |
  | L4 index | full-text and vector projections, keyed by input-manifest digest | **result-deterministic, not byte**: tantivy segment ids are random UUIDs. Indexes are projections and are excluded from byte checks. | `tantivy` (MIT, mature); Qdrant exists |
  | L5 analysis | SQL/dataframe over Parquet; seeded classical ML whose output is a proposal | byte-identical single-threaded | ClickHouse (exists); `linfa` (MIT/Apache, pre-1.0) with `rand_xoshiro`. Deferred until ClickHouse cannot answer a query: `polars` (MIT, heavy, API churn), `datafusion` (Apache, heavy), `duckdb` (MIT, bundled C++). |
  | L6 checks | Descartes, topo verify/gate, link checker, plus a receipt-completeness lint | deterministic | existing |
  | L7 proposals | only items still unresolved after L0–L6, only when the answer changes a defined question; output is proposal files | non-deterministic, recorded as `llm:<model> prompt=sha256:` | existing completers |
  
  **L7 gate (C30).** Each job records a count per tier (claimed by rules, hash, MinHash, small model or LLM). C30's missing test becomes: model calls per verified outcome on a fixed task mix, trended across versions. If deterministic-only matches deterministic-plus-LLM, the LLM stage is deleted (atoms-ingest A/B rule).
  
  ## 3. Running continuously
  
  - **Executor.** Addams. Add one statically linked `lake` binary to the existing `addams-runner` image as a second build stage, so there is no new image. Run it on the `batch_low` class (preemptible, priority −100, scale to zero).
  - **Trigger.** A `critical-crons` line (through the heartbeat wrapper) runs a cheap L0 stat-walk and calls `addams-api submit` with `idempotency_key = blake3(layer, tool version, input-manifest digest)`. Unchanged input gives the same key and no job. Whether `submit` dedupes on the key needs to be **verified** in `addams-api`. Each layer fans out downstream through fd-3 `enqueue` events with the same key scheme, so the DAG is the parent/child job tree.
  - **Incremental.** Only digests that are new or have new rule versions are processed. A rule-version bump invalidates only the rows that rule produced.
  - **Budgets.**
    - `cost_ceiling_usd` in enforce mode for L7, with a ceiling of 0 for L0–L6.
    - `max_bytes` and `max_items` params, with a checkpoint and exit 75 when hit.
    - `activeDeadlineSeconds` caps wall time.
  - **Backpressure.** KEDA `maxReplicaCount` and `queue_depth`. L7 is enqueued only while L0–L3 depth is 0.
  - **Visibility.** One `pipeline_expectations` row per layer, so a stopped layer surfaces as stale. The existing lint enforces the wrapper.
  - **Cadence ("more or less continuously").**
    - L0 every 15 min.
    - L1–L3 on change. Target: a source change produces dedup output within 1 h.
    - L2 near-dup corpus clustering nightly (two-phase, as in atoms-ingest).
    - L4 rebuilt on manifest change, at most nightly.
    - L5 reports daily.
    - L6 on push, plus a nightly cross-repo run.
    - L7 weekly or on demand.
    - With no change, nothing runs beyond the stat-walk.
  
  ### Daemon approach, evaluated
  
  Chatting daemons beat a central scheduler for sub-second streaming, for hot caches (a loaded model) and for watching a laptop tray via inotify. They lose here on five counts:
  - durable state and replay (C27);
  - idempotency;
  - failure visibility;
  - always-on RAM on 8 GiB control-plane nodes (permacomputing);
  - message ordering, which breaks deterministic routing.
  
  Addams is already "ephemeral daemons talking through a durable ledger". **Recommendation: share the message contract, not processes.**
  - **Schema.** One schema, `lake.msg.v1`: `{schema, kind, route_key, rule_id, subject, inputs[{blake3,bytes}], params_digest, producer{tool,version}}`. Canonical JSON: sorted keys, no floats (Descartes I3). Message id = blake3 of the bytes.
  - **Placement and tests.** The schema sits in Praxis as public shape. Rust and Python fixture tests in Dark validate it, the same pattern as permission-evidence.
  - **Routing.** One entry point, `lake route`, reads a routes table (`rule_id → operation`). Anything unmatched goes to an `unrouted` operation that only records it.
  - **Transport.** The Addams ledger in the cluster; the Praxis outbox locally.
  - **Revisit when** a measured latency need appears.
  
  ## 4. Where code lives
  
  - **Dark `packages/rust/lake-core`.** Digest (dual hash, test vectors including Descartes'), manifest, receipt, `lake.msg.v1`, MinHash. Licence MIT/Apache with no private dependencies, so it can move to a public Praxis workspace if a public producer needs it.
  - **Dark `tools/addams-catalog`, renamed or extended to `lake`.** The L0–L3 subcommands and the Parquet writer.
  - **Descartes.** Unchanged. It is invoked at L6, and only its pinned blake3 version is reused, so Descartes stays small.
  - **No third workspace yet.** One implementation per behaviour: hashing lives in lake-core, and Descartes keeps its own canonical node ids because those are a different behaviour.
  
  ## 5. Job families
  
  | Family | Deterministic | Proposal |
  |---|---|---|
  | Organise | L1 path/magic/container rules | L5 seeded clusters; a human names the clusters |
  | Dedup | L2 exact hash; verified Jaccard | near-dup pairs to the review queue |
  | Align (entity resolution) | `dark.dedup` blocking | its LLM clusters (L7), applied as reversible writes |
  | Correlate | exact-key joins (LEI, CIK, Message-ID) | fuzzy joins (L5/L7) |
  | Themes | lexicon/rule tags (L3/L5) | embeddings + linfa (L5); LLM (L7) |
  | Spam | rules, known-spam hashes (L1/L2) | small model (L5) |
  | Links | L6 checker | argument rewrites (continuous-verification "optimise") |
  
  ## 6. Worked case: spam
  
  **The two stores.**
  - Gmail, with Google's filter.
  - Inbound mail that `tools/mail-sync` mirrors from R2/comms into Stalwart, with no filter (rspamd was retired; `train_rspamd.py` has no live target).
  
  **Witnesses, by strength.**
  - Gmail `SPAM` / not-`SPAM` labels are testimony, recorded with the snapshot of when they were observed. They are not ground truth.
  - `triage.spam_decisions` (Sloane's clicks) is the stronger witness.
  - `triage.auto_spam_decisions` is our own model's output and is **excluded from training** (circular).
  
  **Join.** RFC 5322 `Message-ID`, with a fallback of blake3 over the normalised `From`, `Date` and `Subject`. Unmatched messages are reported, never guessed.
  
  **Tiers.**
  1. Rules on `Authentication-Results` (SPF/DKIM/DMARC). A `List-Unsubscribe` header marks bulk mail, which is not the same as spam.
  2. Exact hash of the normalised body against the known-spam set.
  3. MinHash LSH against Gmail-labelled campaign clusters.
  4. linfa Naive Bayes or logistic regression over hashed tokens, fixed seed. Ready-made alternative: re-stand rspamd's Bayes.
  
  **Evaluation.**
  - Split by time, with each near-dup campaign confined to one side so campaigns do not leak across the split.
  - Report a confusion matrix and Cohen's κ against Gmail and against Sloane's clicks, plus the claim rate per tier.
  - Disagreements go into Sloane's 25-minute review batch.
  
  **Access needed.** The existing `get_gmail()` connector, calling `messages.list` with `includeSpamTrash=true` and `format=metadata` (labelIds plus Message-ID only, no bodies). Today `gmail_sync.py` sets no `includeSpamTrash`, so Gmail-spam messages are probably never synced. The current token's scope (`gmail.metadata` or `gmail.readonly`) must be confirmed. I propose no new credential.
  
  **Data placement.** Everything stays in Dark and runs in the cluster only. Signatures use per-enclave keyed hashing (Descartes T1: bare digests leak which messages exist). Weights trained on mail are private. Only aggregates leave.
  
  ## 7. Resilience
  
  | Failure | Behaviour |
  |---|---|
  | Node lost | The lease expires and the job is reclaimed. Outputs are write-once, and a receipt is written only after readback, so partial writes are orphaned, not corrupting. |
  | Network or R2 down | Exit 75, retry. Laptop runs are fully offline (addams-catalog already is). |
  | Model provider gone | The L7 queue grows; L0–L6 are unaffected. A `deterministic_only` flag is the normal mode, not an emergency one. |
  | Source gone | Pins are kept by digest in the lake and storage boxes. `topo verify` flags drift. Nothing is silently re-fetched. |
  | Cluster gone | The same static binary over Parquet on a laptop; the `duckdb` CLI stands in for ClickHouse (it must be declared in a Brewfile per R36). |
  | Restore | Rebuild L1–L5 from L0 pins plus recipes in a clean namespace. Everything except L4 must be byte-identical. |
  
  ## 8. First slice (one week)
  
  1. **Days 1–2: lake-core.** Dual digest with vector tests, receipt, canonical JSON.
  2. **Days 2–3: extend addams-catalog.** Add `near-dup` and `export --parquet`.
  3. **Day 4: run it.**
     - In the cluster, as a `batch_low` Addams job, over the Praxis topology pins (fetched in-job by `topo.py pin`, the gated path) and Descartes `sources/`.
     - The same binary locally over the tray, with PII skipped as it is now. The tray stays local and its sync path is unchanged.
  4. **Day 5: one analysis in ClickHouse.** Near-duplicate clusters across bodies (for example shared terms boilerplate), joined to `gate.tsv` verdicts.
  
  **Acceptance tests**
  1. Two runs, on arm64 and amd64, produce identical sha256 for every Parquet file and receipt.
  2. The tray exact-dup counts reproduce the previous run: 15,531 groups, 52,082 files.
  3. On a planted near-dup fixture: recall ≥0.95 at J≥0.8, and every reported pair has verified J≥0.8.
  4. Every output has a complete receipt (lint).
  5. L0–L3 pass with egress denied; only `topo.py` fetches.
  6. Unchanged input produces no new Addams job; touching one file reprocesses only that file.
  7. A gate-denied URL never appears in any output.
  8. Zero quarantine paths in any Parquet file.
  9. Stopping the cron raises a stale expectation.
  
  ## 9. Risks and open decisions for Sloane
  
  1. **Primary hash.** BLAKE3 (matches Descartes) or SHA-256 (matches `catalog.addams_items`, addams-api). Proposed: compute both and make BLAKE3 the id. Also choose `pure` (portable, slower) or SIMD.
  2. **MinHash.** Pin `gaoya` exactly, or keep about 100 lines in-house. A dependency bump that changes signatures would silently break byte-identical rebuilds.
  3. **Custody.** Lake placement and receipts for topology pins are not yet established (producer contract). Until they are, the slice treats them as staging.
  4. **`lake-core` stays private for now** or goes into a public workspace now.
  5. **Stays Python:**
     - `topo.py` (stdlib, tested; it is the gate, and porting it risks weakening the gate);
     - the exclusions feeder and `check_exclusion_sources.py`;
     - `dark.dedup`, gmail_sync, `process_events`, the heartbeat wrapper;
     - the catalog sync, until the schema review's Tier 1 items close.
     Moves to Rust: only the hot loops (hash, shingle, Parquet).
  6. **Gmail.** Confirm the token scope and approve `includeSpamTrash` metadata reads.
  7. **Daemons.** Accept "shared contract, Addams transport" or name a latency need that justifies a long-lived process.
  8. **C30.** Approve the per-tier counters as C30's test.
  
  ### Critical Files for Implementation
  - dark-monocloud `origin/codex/dark-buildkite:tools/addams-catalog/src/main.rs`
  - dark-monocloud `origin/main:tools/addams-api/TASK_IO_CONTRACT.md`
  - dark-monocloud `origin/main:infra/k3s/jobs/scaledjob-batch-low.yaml` and `infra/containers/rust/addams-runner/Dockerfile`
  - /Users/srvo/dev/praxis/resources/topologies/scripts/topo.py
  - /Users/srvo/dev/descartes/Cargo.toml (blake3 pin) and dark-monocloud `origin/main:infra/containers/python/email/src/gmail_sync.py`
